Against GL1 the same numbers as sessions 9–12: dm6 1454 / 135 / 496 / 2533 / 1404 / 152 / 117 / 1 / 2437, dm6fx 0 / 4 / 605 / 899882 (view 3, also session 12's), m2l1 4252–4260 / 753, dm4 843 / 1694 / 323 / 521, obj3 11389 / 16 / 14151.
  - `port/skinab.sh` (build "installed"), whole bench: 99,506 batches, 19.6 M vertices, **0 differences in every field** (the same counts as session 11).
  - The build passes; `port/glcheck.sh` is clean.
- **Looked at and left:**
  - the world's BSP walk (`R_RecursiveWorldNode`, 0.014 ms): classic Q3 code, everything inlined, memory-bound; omaha's exact world culls (§4b: flat PVS leaf list, 8-leaf group culls, per-face bounding spheres) are the candidates, kept for later with a screenshot diff each;
  - the skeletor's bone transforms (`TIKI_GetFrameInternal`, 0.011 ms): shared with the game code, outside the renderer;
  - `RB_SkelMesh`'s mesh search (a `TIKI_GetSkel` call and a list walk per surface; omaha's "mesh-index table at model load"): a few µs at most.

**Phase 5a, closed (session 13).** The ≤ 0.25 ms target is met: **0.234 ms** work per frame unlocked (median 0.236), down from 0.41 at the start of 5a, against GL1's 0.95 in the same sitting. The GPU is ~10% busy (~0.1 ms per frame): the frame is CPU-bound. Where the remaining ~0.23 ms goes (session 13's last profile, `s13all2`: 3140 frame-work samples over ~13,500 frames ≈ 0.232 ms per frame, the bench's figure):
- **Metal and the driver, ~17%** (+3% ring copies): AGX, IOGPU/IOKit, the kernel and objc under the backend. That is the per-draw state emission in `drawIndexedPrimitives` (~58 draws per frame) and the fixed per-frame costs (`MB_BeginPass`: command buffer and scene encoder; `mbEndFrame`: commit, the final pass, present, the frame-slot wait; together ~7%). Fewer draws means merging batches, which changes the draw order (not bit-identical). The fixed costs come with the frame structure.
- **Engine-side, ~42%:** cgame's own work (~18%), the UI widgets, the client frame, the SDL pump and OpenAL (~19%), and the skeletor's animation called from the front end (`TIKI_GetFrameInternal`, ~5%). None of it is renderer code.
- **Renderer CPU, ~38%:** the front end without the skeletor (~17%: world walk, entity surfaces, sort) and the backend's own code (~21%: the surface functions, the iterators, `MB_PrepareDraw`). What was cheap to cut exactly has been cut; omaha's exact world culls are the next candidates (above).

Session 14 result (5b, p99 frame time vs GL1, the user away):
- **The per-frame work log** (ae894bcf, client only, timedemo only): `CL_SetCGameTime` records each timedemo frame's work, µs since the last timedemo frame minus the frame limiter's wait added since then (`com_timedemoWaitUsec`), in `clc.timeDemoWorkUsec` next to `timeDemoDurations`. `cl_timedemoLog` writes it as a second column after a `# frame ms, frame work usec` comment line. The mean of the column matches the "timedemo work" figure (0.2357 vs 0.236 ms). No effect on pacing or usercmds: one `Com_Microseconds()` per timedemo frame.
- **`port/bench.sh run`** prints p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max of the work µs (nearest rank) at the end of each run line, and a last line over all the runs of the call.
- **The `gl` reference carries the log:** `port/build.sh gl` applies the client hunks of `GL1_PATCHES` (ae894bcf) to the `GL1_REF` export (c9dddadd, unchanged); the export is redone when the patch list changes (`.gl1-ref` holds both). The renderer of the reference is untouched.
- **Sitting** (screen unlocked throughout; Metal at ae894bcf and GL1, alternating one run each, 8 runs each; 2397 logged frames per run). Work µs per frame:
  | Build | p50 | p95 | **p99** | **p99.9** | max | mean work |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Metal, pooled | 212 | 454 | **739** | **1536** | 7880 | 0.237 ms |
  | GL1, pooled | 848 | 1681 | **2318** | **3490** | 10251 | 0.914 ms |
  - Per run: Metal p99 671 / 688 / 670 / 773 / 669 / 1005 / 859 / 709, p99.9 1341 / 1391 / 1200 / 2453 / 1218 / 1578 / 1601 / 1073; GL1 p99 2503 / 2024 / 2433 / 2363 / 1910 / 2469 / 2031 / 2702, p99.9 3375 / 2695 / 3370 / 3570 / 2863 / 3541 / 3013 / 3833. The worst Metal run's p99 (1005 µs) is below GL1's best (1910 µs) and near GL1's median frame (848 µs). Metal's pooled p99 is **3.1× lower** than GL1's, its p99.9 2.3× lower.
  - Logs: `build.noindex/s14/sit1/` (`summary.txt`, the per-run frame logs and game logs).
- **The slow frames** (temporary timers around the client frame's parts, cgame's calls into sound/registration/traces, the renderer's front end/backend, pipeline and image creation, and file opens; never committed, kept as `build.noindex/s14/diag.patch`):
  - **The frames that are slow in every run** (1343, 1302, 2252–2253, 2392, 713, 296, 318, …: +0.6–1.0 ms on Metal, +0.7–1.2 ms on GL1, at the same frames) **are failed sound opens.** The demo triggers ~83 dialogue lines per run that aren't in the retail data (`sound/dialogue/generic/a/death/dpl_death_02i.wav`, `…/damage/dpl_damage_12i.wav`, …; GL1 logs the same 83 `WARNING: Failed to open sound`). Each trigger costs 250–400 µs on the main thread: `S_OPENAL_StartSound` → `openal_channel_two_d_stream::set_sfx` → `S_CodecGetSound` tries the WAV, MP3, OGG and Opus codecs in turn, each `FS_FOpenFileRead` misses in the two directory search paths, and each miss runs `FS_CorrectCase` (`access` + `opendir`/`readdir` of the parent), then the warning is printed. Nothing remembers the failure. Both volumes are case-insensitive APFS, so `FS_CorrectCase`'s directory scan can never find anything there. 20 of the 23 frames above one run's p99 had ≥ 150 µs in cgame's `S_StartSound`; server-sent sounds hit the same path from the demo parse (`demo` 266–521 µs at frames 296, 864, 2252). Engine-side (client sound code and `qcommon/files.cpp`), not renderer: the follow-up is in §6.
  - **Frame 0** (5–8 ms Metal, 3–10 ms GL1): the first timedemo frame. ~5 ms is `IN_Frame` + `Com_EventLoop` (the first in-game frame after the load), ~0.4 ms the first draw's registrations (`global/lensflaredefs.txt`, a HUD texture, 3 shaders).
  - **Metal-side:** an occasional explosion frame (713–715 in some runs) shows a front-end spike (0.45 ms) and then a frame-slot wait in `RE_EndFrame` (0.2–1.1 ms): the GPU falling two frames behind on a heavy-overdraw frame. `r_metalPresentStats`: ~0.001 ms of slot wait per frame on average, 0 ms waiting for drawables. No pipeline was created and no image uploaded during the timedemo (first-use costs are all at load). The other p99.9 frames (e.g. 1087–1093 in one run, 4.4 / 2.3 / 2.2 ms) are not repeatable: scheduling noise.
  - In MOHAA the 2D draws flush the pending render commands (`Draw_StretchPic` → `R_IssuePendingRenderCommands`), so the 3D backend runs inside cgame's draw, and `RE_EndFrame` only presents (~5–15 µs).
- **Optional world culls, measured and skipped:** the bench adds ~478 world faces per world walk, ~119 of them (25%) entirely outside the frustum by their point bounds (temporary counter, reverted). A per-face cull would save their sort and backend work but costs a sphere test on all 478; the estimate is ≤ ~2 µs net per frame, under what the bench resolves, and the whole BSP walk is 14 µs. omaha's flat leaf list / 8-leaf group culls bound the gain at a few µs more. Not done; the sound fix above is worth ~100× more at p99.
- Build passes; `port/glcheck.sh` clean. No renderer code changed (so no regress/skinab run needed).

Session 14, continued: **5c, the engine-side p99 tail** (the user moved it ahead of Phase 7, §3.1 #8). Both changes are outside the renderer and keep the output the same:
- **Confirmed:** the dialogue lines are missing from the retail data. `sound/dialogue/Generic/A/damage/` in Pak4 holds only `dpl_damage_13i.wav`, and there are no `death` files at all; the aliases reference more variants than the game ships, so the original game fails on them too. Streamed sounds are never checked at registration (`S_LoadSound` returns at once for them), so every start opened the file.
- **Change 1** (9e1c543f, `snd_openal_new.cpp`, `snd_dma_new.cpp`, `snd_local_new.h`): `openal_channel_two_d_stream::set_sfx` marks the sfx `SFX_FLAG_STREAM_FAILED` when `S_CodecLoad` fails, and returns at once on later starts (with the same side effects: `stop()`, the channel fields). `S_BeginRegistration` clears the flag on every sfx, so each map load (after any download) searches again. cgame calls `S_RegisterSound` on every trigger, so the flag can't be reset there. Warnings per run: 83 → 29 (once per missing file per map).
- **Change 2** (0d0b3148, `qcommon/files.cpp` `check_filecase`): every file read from a pk3 first misses in the two directory search paths, and each miss ran `FS_CorrectCase`: `access()` on every path component, then an `opendir`/`readdir` scan of the first missing component's parent. On a case-insensitive volume `access()` already ignores case, so the scan can't find anything: `check_filecase` now asks that directory's own volume (`pathconf(dir, _PC_CASE_SENSITIVE)`) and skips the scan when it says 0. Exact across mount points; case-sensitive volumes still scan. It also speeds up every streamed sound that does exist (each start opens its file).
- **Bench** (screen unlocked; work µs per frame, pooled):
  | Sitting | Build | p50 | p95 | **p99** | **p99.9** | mean work | wall per run |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | sit2 (8 runs each, alternating) | metalref (696cec0a, before) | 207 | 411 | **692** | **1316** | 0.2278 ms | 13.716 s |
  | | metal (both changes) | 210 | 375 | **497** | **745** | 0.2221 ms | 13.619 s |
  | sit3 (6 runs each, alternating) | metalref (9e1c543f, change 1 only) | 214 | 390 | **594** | **1006** | 0.2298 ms | 13.694 s |
  | | metal (both changes) | 211 | 373 | **498** | **996** | 0.2226 ms | 13.608 s |
  - sit2 per run: metalref p99 653–735, metal 446–543 (**every metal run below every metalref run**); p99.9 1186–1747 vs 581–921. The frames that were slow in every run now sit near their neighbours: frame 1343 1270 → 602 µs (median of 8), 1302 926 → 452, 2252 999 → 508, 2392 1002 → 422, 296 824 → 418.
  - sit3 isolates change 2: p99 594 → 498 (every run below: 485–512 vs 574–656), mean work −0.007 ms, and ~0.09 s less per run (startup and map load; wall time without each sitting's first round). p99.9 scatters ±150 µs between sittings (745 vs 996 for the same build): compare it within one sitting.
  - Profile (`port/prof.sh`, 20 s, 8 loops each, the loops include demo reloads): `S_OPENAL_StartSound` 155 → 25 samples, `FS_CorrectCase` 381 → 54 (`build.noindex/prof/s14snd.txt` → `s14snd2.txt`).
  - Logs: `build.noindex/s14/sit2/`, `sit3/` (`summary.txt` has each run's wall time and warning count).
- **Against GL1** (sit1, the same bench): Metal's p99 is now ~500 µs vs GL1's ~2300, **4.7× lower**; p99.9 ~750–1000 vs ~3500.
- What's left above Metal's p99: the 29 first triggers of missing sounds (now ~0.2 ms each: the codec probes still miss in every search path once per map), frame 0 (the first in-game frame), and the occasional explosion frame's frame-slot wait.
- Build passes (game and `omohaaded`); `port/glcheck.sh` clean. No renderer code changed.

Session 26 result (5d, exclusive fullscreen's and the borderless window's lost frames; the user at the machine at the start, asked: "Go ahead" for the runs that take the cursor, "Lost frames, no me"; then away, the screen locked once after ~20 min without input and the user unlocked it; commit on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **Reproduced and explained** (§2, "lost frames, and late presents"): with another application drawing on the other display (the Claude app on the 4K, redrawing at 60/s while a session works), the WindowServer flips our direct-to-display frames late (GPU done → on screen p95 1.3 → 3.8 ms), the next frame's drawable is still on screen, and ~13% of frames are skipped (their input shown a frame later: p95 ~6 ms). A still window over the 4K, or my own 60 Hz layer animation there, removes it; GPU-rendered frames there cause it (`d2gpu`). The Space never showed it because macOS blanks the other display for a fullscreen Space on this Mac ("Displays have separate Spaces" off). Game Mode was on throughout.
- **3 drawables rejected** again: the flips still queue (p95 4.6–4.9), and +0.6 ms p50 when nothing is in the way.
- **Late presents** (`r_metalLatePresent`, default 1; §3.2 "Added in session 26"; `tr_mtl.m`: `MB_EncodeFinal`, `MB_PostLatePresent`, `MB_PresentLate`, `MB_CancelLatePresent`, the drawable thread): a frame without a drawable is presented by the drawable thread as soon as one comes back, if it can still be committed 2 ms before the next frame's end. Exclusive with the Claude app visible: **1.97 / 4.23 / 4.63 and 1.99 / 4.35 / 4.76 ms** (frame end → on screen p50 / p95 / p99; before 1.93–2.01 / 5.99–6.28 / 6.46–7.07), 0–0.1% of frames shown by a later one (before 11–15%); the borderless window 1.92 / 4.17 / 4.54 (before 1.91–2.23 / 5.83–6.45 / 6.50–7.33); the clean cases and 1000 fps unchanged. A traced run puts the probe's present times on the display's swaps (1683 of 1689 within 2 µs; 248 swaps/s instead of 217).
- **Bench, ABBA ×2 each** (`build.noindex/s26/abL.sh` / `abL2.sh`: the same install with `r_metalLatePresent 0` and `1`; unlocked; the window bench at ~1000 fps): the first version without the deadline (late presents on ~40% of the bench's frames): off 0.228 / 0.238 / 0.225 / 0.213 ms work (mean 0.226), pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 215/387/513/1070/4626 µs; on 0.225 / 0.246 / 0.229 / 0.219 (0.230), 216/392/533/1107/7002. The final version (13–16 late presents per run): **on 0.221 / 0.223 / 0.215 / 0.223 (0.2205), 210/367/487/776/5443 µs; off 0.222 / 0.224 / 0.222 / 0.225 (0.223), 212/372/492/707/6919: equal.** (The scratch log was compiled in, inactive, for these; the final binary differs only by its removal.)
- **Checks:** build; `port/glcheck.sh` clean; the seal verifies; the final binary in exclusive with the Claude app visible (1286 frames without a drawable at their end, 1203 presented late) and with vsync (`r_swapInterval 1`: 3 drawables, every frame presented, no late presents). No rendering change (the final pass is the same pass, moved into `MB_EncodeFinal`), so no fixed views.
- **Tools** (scratch, `build.noindex/s26/`): `run.sh` (one `MOUSE=0` run: mode × what is on the 4K, the scratch log analysed by `dl.py`), `batch.sh` (several, stops when the screen is locked or the input was used), `d2.m`/`d2gpu.m` (the 4K: `cover`, `busy`, `small`, `gpuN`), `dl.py` (frame end → on screen, the skipped frames' drawable delay, the drawable's return after its successor's flip, commit → GPU → screen), `tr_mtl-dlog.m` (the backend with the scratch log, `OMM_DLOG=<file>`: the prototype before the deadline and the final polish; diff it against `tr_mtl.m` to carry the log over), `abL.sh`/`abL2.sh` (ABBA bench of a cvar).

Session 27 result (5e, the pipeline items; the user away at the start (8 min idle), back once mid-session and asked before the bench: "Run them now" for the runs that take the cursor, "Not now" for the parts that need them; commits on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **The pipeline cache was mostly broken, and silently.** `serializeToURL:` fails ("Failed to generate machO … expecting 'fragment' stage in pipeline no. N") on an archive that was read from a file and then got pipelines added, for some sets of pipelines: reproduced outside the game (`build.noindex/s27/archtest`): an archive of `final` + `0x0e001` read back, plus `0x0e089` or `0x0e005`, fails; plus `0x0e003` or `0x2e001` works; a dozen additions failed every time; with or without the depth query pipeline. After a failed serialize, the same archive object writes only what was added after the failure (session 19's "1 pipeline instead of 13"). This client routes the renderer's `PRINT_WARNING` to `Com_DPrintf` (developer only), so nobody saw it: every bench run in session 26's logs found 2 of its 15 listed pipelines in the archive and compiled the other 13 at startup (cheap only because the system shader cache had them). `metalref` shows it plainly: a menu-only run, then the bench twice: "pipeline cache not written" on both (with `developer 1`), the archive stuck at the menu's pipelines, 10 created at their first draw on every run.
- **A fresh archive always serializes** (archtest: 5, 24 and 45 pipelines, the query among them, every one found after reading it back). So the cache now writes a new archive holding every pipeline the process created (their descriptors are kept), never the one it read; only when the run compiled a pipeline (`mb_archiveDirty`), ~30 ms at the quit or `vid_restart`. A failed write leaves the old archive and prints a visible line; the keys file is written whenever the list grew, even then (a listed key the archive lacks is compiled up front by the next run, never in play). `MB_CACHE_VERSION` 2, now part of the file names' hash, retires version 1's archives. **The query pipeline stays in the archive:** a fresh archive with it reads back fine, and the failure came with or without it.
- **Pipelines ahead of their first draw** (`R_PrecacheShaderPipelines` in `FinishShader`, `R_PrecacheState` next to `R_State`, `mbPrecachePipeline` / `mbPrecacheFinish` in the backend): each shader, when it is loaded, describes the state each of its stages draws with, as the stage iterators set it up: the generic iterator per stage (blend, alpha test, one or two texture units), the vertex-lit fast path also with GPU skinning, the lightmapped multitexture path, the sky's outer box; plus ProjectDlightTexture's two passes (models under dynamic lights) at startup. The backend takes a missing pipeline from the archive at once, else compiles it with `newRenderPipelineStateWithDescriptor:completionHandler:` on Metal's threads (up to 64 at a time). A draw that needs one still compiling waits for that one only; `RE_EndRegistration` (the end of `CL_InitCGame`) waits for the rest, so none is compiled in play. The startup's keys list goes the same way (its misses compile in parallel). `R_State` is unchanged: a first version moved its blend switch into a helper, which stayed an out-of-line call per blend change, so the factors became a table in `R_PrecacheState`.
- **Measured:**
  - archtest, variants the system shader cache had never seen: 5–30 ms each one at a time on the calling thread; 8 at once with the async call: submitted in ~2 ms, all done 37 ms later on Metal's threads. A process's first pipeline call costs ~10 ms (the compiler service's connection), the first specialization of `mb_stage_fs` ~10 ms.
  - Empty cache, the bench demo (dm6): 16 of 17 pipelines compiled ahead of their first draw (the slowest done 0.6 ms after its submission; 0.0 ms waited at the end of the load). The one created at its first draw is `0xe548`, the console's untextured box (`DrawBox`; the console and menu only, never predicted; the keys list has it after one run). Before (`metalref`): all 14 at their first draw.
  - Warm: 17 of 17 listed pipelines from the archive at startup in 1.2 ms (before: 2 of 15, the rest compiled), none compiled ahead or at a first draw, nothing written. A menu-only archive (6) + the bench map (11 new): written, the next run 17 of 17 from the archive. dm/mohdm4 after dm6: 1 new variant compiled ahead (0.4 ms, nothing waited), written, the next run 18 of 18; m2l1 and obj/obj_team3 after that brought nothing new.
  - The bench's keys: `0e001 0e003 0e005 0e007 0e089 0e119 0e11b 0e149 0e14b 0e189 0e309 0e459 0e548 0e549 0e54b 2e001 2e005` (`0e11b` and `0e14b`, two multitextured blends, are predicted but not drawn there).
- **Bench, ABBA ×4** (`build.noindex/s27/ab.sh`, each flavor with its own pipeline cache: `metal` and `metalref` delete each other's files; `metalref` = ff5691e2, the session's start; unlocked, nobody on the input, load 2.5–4.2): `metal` 0.217 / 0.215 / 0.218 / 0.216 / 0.217 / 0.218 / 0.217 / 0.217 ms work (mean 0.2169), pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9 207/359/470/667 µs; `metalref` 0.214 / 0.221 / 0.214 / 0.218 / 0.217 / 0.217 / 0.215 / 0.217 (mean 0.2166), 207/362/479/623 µs. **Equal.** (The first version, with the helper in `R_State`, 3 rounds earlier: 0.2188 vs 0.2168 over 12 runs each, within the noise; the helper went anyway.)
- **Checks:** build (the install signs and checks the seal); `port/glcheck.sh` clean; renderer warnings none new. Rendering can't change (the same descriptors, created earlier), so no fixed views.
- **Tools** (scratch, `build.noindex/s27/`): `archtest.m` (the archive outside the game: `open <file|new>`, `get <key>`, `query`, `final`, `save <file>` (version 1's write), `fresh <file>` (a new archive of everything created), `async <keys> end`), `ab.sh` (ABBA bench, one pipeline cache per flavor in `cache/<flavor>/`, numbering continued from the files in `ab/`).

Session 28 result (5f, the GPU's wake-up before each frame; the user at the machine at the start, asked: "Run them now" for the runs that take the cursor, "Not now" for their parts; then away, the screen locked ~20 min after their last input, asked again: "Unlocked, run them"; commits on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **What the ~0.37 ms from commit to GPU start is: the GPU powering up after idling.** Outside the game (`build.noindex/s28/gpustart`: no window; per period one command buffer, a 1920×1080 render pass with ~0.16 ms of GPU work, committed 0.25 ms after the "frame start", 2 in flight; paced exactly by sleeping to 1.5 ms before the start and spinning, because a sleeping thread woke up to ~0.8 ms late): commit → GPU start p50 **0.12 ms at a 1 ms period, 0.33 ms at 2 ms, 0.57 ms at 4 ms, 0.58 at 8 ms** (p95 0.17 / 0.40 / 0.70 / 0.72); the minimum ~0.06–0.1 ms at every period. After ~1.3–1.5 ms without work the GPU goes to sleep, and the next commit waits ~0.3–0.5 ms for it. The bench never shows it (~1000 fps: 0.124 ms in `r_metalPresentStats`); at 250 fps every frame paid it (in play, the Space: 0.60–0.64 ms mean).
- **A tiny command buffer ahead of the frame wakes it.** gpustart at 4 ms, the frame's GPU end in µs after its start (committed at ~260): no kick ~935–1019; a blit committed at the frame's start ~750; 0.3 ms before it ~680; 0.8 ms ~640; 1.0 ms ~612; 1.3 ms ~590; 1.6 ms ~780; 2 ms ~878 (the GPU has gone back to sleep). The kick's own commit → start is ~0.5 ms (it pays the wake-up instead of the frame). Blit, compute and render kicks do the same; an empty command buffer never reaches the GPU ("done" in 0.3 µs) and wakes nothing. A kick costs 16–33 µs of CPU (p50), a process's first one 0.4–1.2 ms (the driver's first blit or command setup).
- **In the game** (`re.WakeGPU`, §3.2 "Added in session 28"): the renderer commits a 4-byte blit fill when the engine starts a frame, unless something was committed within the last millisecond. Placement, measured with temporary counters (`build.noindex/s28/dbg.patch`, never committed; GPU start after commit by the kick's lead before the frame's commit, the Space at 250 fps): lead 0.2–0.3 ms → 0.35 ms; 0.4–0.8 ms → 0.23–0.28; 0.9–1.6 ms → 0.29–0.38; 1.7–2.0 ms → 0.37–0.55 (dozing again); no kick 0.66. A first version kicked inside the frame limiter's wait, in its last millisecond: only 46% of the frames got one (the limiter's 3 ms `select` often wakes past the frame's tick, and its last-millisecond branch never runs), and the ones that did came 1.4–1.9 ms ahead of the commit. A kick right after the wait, before `IN_Frame`, lands 0.5–0.6 ms ahead (the frame's pump → commit) on every frame: the best spot, and one call site.
- **Measured** (`build.noindex/s28/run.sh`: `MOUSE=0 LATSTATS=2` runs of `port/latency.sh`, dm/mohdm6, `com_maxfps 250`, vsync off, 15 s; `fr.py` pools every frame's commit, GPU-done and present times from `cl_latencyStats 2`; `r_metalWakeGPU` 0/1 alternating, the same binary; unlocked, nobody on the input, load 2.1–4.7). µs, p50 / p95 / p99, 4 runs each pooled (19 k frames) in the Space, 2 each in exclusive:

  | Mode | `r_metalWakeGPU` | commit → GPU start (mean) | commit → GPU done | commit → on screen |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | the Space | 0 | 597–642 | 1162 / 1336 / 1387 | 2359 / 2652 / 3528 |
  | the Space | **1** | **351–360** | **740 / 876 / 952** | **2001 / 2305 / 2451** |
  | exclusive, the Claude app on the 4K | 0 | 530–558 | 1074 / 1336 / 1404 | 2382 / 4606 / 5353 |
  | exclusive, the Claude app on the 4K | **1** | **340–346** | **730 / 897 / 1042** | **2072 / 4529 / 5263** |
  | the Space, `com_maxfps 0` (~1000 fps) | 0 / 1 | 133 / 130 | 506 / 543 / 589; 502 / 540 / 590 | 1614 / 1866 / 1966; 1618 / 1878 / 1962 (10 wake-ups in the run) |
  | the Space, vsync (`r_swapInterval 1`), 1 run each | 0 / 1 | 734 / 412 | 1226 / 1369 / 1393; 757 / 860 / 1206 | 6529 / 7716 / 7777; 6537 / 7702 / 7761 |

  - **The frame is on screen ~0.35 ms sooner at 250 fps** (p50 and p95, the Space and exclusive alike), and its GPU work is done ~0.42 ms sooner. Every run with it was below every run without it. The GPU's own work also got shorter (0.45–0.46 vs 0.46–0.48 ms per frame). The commit intervals are unchanged (p50 4.01 vs 4.03 ms): pacing and input sampling are untouched; the call costs ~20–30 µs, between the wait and `IN_Frame`.
  - At ~1000 fps the GPU never sleeps and nothing is committed (10 wake-ups in a run, at the load). With vsync the GPU is done sooner, but the frame still waits for its refresh (the vsync queue: the low-latency vsync item of §4b).
  - From session 23's Space numbers (pump → present 2.15 / 2.4 / 2.5 ms), this puts pump → present at ~1.8 ms; the probe needs mouse input to show it (the synthetic-mouse check, with the user).
- **Bench** (`build.noindex/s28/ab.sh`, ABBA ×4 twice; `metalref` = 824f3acd, the session's start; unlocked, nobody on the input, load 2.4–3.8), ms work per frame and pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max µs:
  - first set: `metal` 0.225 (its first run after a build) / 0.218 / 0.216 / 0.219, 207/374/501/1367/7573; `metalref` 0.214 / 0.215 / 0.216 / 0.215, 205/360/470/651/5792. `metal`'s first frame was 6.0–7.6 ms against 4.1–5.8: a bench run makes one wake-up, at its first frame after the load, and it was the process's first blit (0.4–1.2 ms in gpustart). So the renderer now commits one when it starts.
  - second set, with that: `metal` 0.218 / 0.220 / 0.226 / 0.217 (mean 0.2203), 210/370/490/909/5595; `metalref` 0.215 / 0.215 / 0.220 / 0.217 (0.2168), 207/357/476/864/7466. First frames 2.9–5.6 vs 4.4–7.5 ms.
  - the same binary with `r_metalWakeGPU` 0 / 1 / 1 / 0: 0.215 / 0.218 / 0.216 / 0.214; pooled 0: 204/360/469/723, 1: 207/362/474/661.
  - Without frame 0 (µs per frame): `metal` 215.6 (runs 2–8 without one 224.5), `metalref` 213.2 (without one 218.4); wake 0 vs 1: 213.0 vs 214.8. **+~2 µs (1%), inside the runs' spread** (212–218 for one build): the bench makes one wake-up per run, and the per-frame check is a clock read and a compare. p99 equal. Not chased further; watch it in the next bench.
- **Checks:** build (game, `omohaaded`; the install signs and checks the seal); `port/glcheck.sh` clean. No rendering change (a blit into the renderer's own 16-byte buffer), so no fixed views.
- **Tools** (scratch, `build.noindex/s28/`): `gpustart.m` (commit → GPU start outside the game: `gpustart <period_us> <frames> <cpu_us> <draws> <none|empty|blit|compute|render> [lead_us]`, prints percentiles, the timeline's medians and the kick's CPU cost; build line in its header), `run.sh <name> <space|excl|win> <r_metalWakeGPU> [engine args]` (one `MOUSE=0 LATSTATS=2` run with `r_metalPresentStats`), `fr.py <logs>` (pooled per-frame commit → GPU done / on screen / commit intervals), `ab.sh` (session 27's, with this session's `ab/` and `cache/`), `dbg.patch` / `tr_mtl-dbg.m` (the temporary counters: GPU start by kick lead, kicked or not, with or without a drawable), `batch.sh` / `batch2.sh` and their outputs.

Plan (session 1):
- Latency work from §4b (frames-in-flight tuning, GCMouse raw input, the client timing redesign: precise pacing by default + the optional decoupled command/render mode + the percentile cap suggestion + low-latency vsync, activity/QoS/Game Mode, audio latency), measured with the latency probe.
- Measure first (bench + `trace` + `sample`/Time Profiler), one change at a time, each recorded here with before/after numbers. Candidates:
  - Static world geometry in GPU-resident buffers (no per-frame re-tessellation/copy).
  - Batching by shader+lightmap; sort keys to minimize PSO/state changes; fewer render passes.
  - GPU skinning (compute) for TIKI; GPU tcMod/tcGen/rgbGen/deforms for the common cases.
  - Faster `RB_Light_Real` sphere lighting.
  - BC texture compression for memory; async texture loading at map load.
  - Metal 4 (argument tables, residency sets, `MTL4CommandQueue`) where measured.
  - Frame pacing (present timing / CAMetalDisplayLink).
- Engine frame floor: `Com_Frame` pads frames to 1 ms with an integer-ms clock, which caps fps near 1000. Making it µs-precise changes how `com_maxfps` behaves online (frame pacing feeds usercmd timing and movement feel), so **ask the user before touching it**.
- **Done when:** the bench work time is ≤ 0.25 ms/frame or the remaining cost is documented as engine-side, p99 frame time is lower than GL1, input→present latency is measured lower than the Phase 2 numbers (and as low as the display mode allows), and the fixed views (`port/shot.sh pair`) show no visual change.
  - *Status after session 14:* work time **met** (0.234 ms, the rest documented above); fixed views **met** (every change, `regress base-s9`); **p99 vs GL1 met** (session 14: work p99 739 µs vs GL1 2318, p99.9 1536 vs 3490, pooled over 8 runs each, unlocked; after 5c ~500 µs, 4.7× below GL1); **latency open** (after Phase 6, on the native input layer, §3.1 #8; `port/latency.sh` takes the mouse: with the user). *Session 26:* exclusive fullscreen and the borderless window no longer lose frames while another application draws on the other display (5d, late presents); the probe check against the Phase 2 numbers with the synthetic mouse still needs the user. *Session 28:* the GPU is woken when a frame starts (5f): commit → on screen ~0.35 ms shorter at 250 fps (Space 2.36 → 2.00 ms p50).

### Phase 6: Native AppKit/GameController (replace SDL2). **Status: done (6a, the survey and design, done in session 18, 2026-10-18; 6b, the application and the window, done in session 19, 2026-10-18; 6c, the keyboard and text, done in session 20, 2026-10-18; 6d, the mouse, done in session 21, 2026-10-18; 6e, the rest, done in session 22, 2026-10-18; 6f's synthetic half, the latency sitting, done in session 23, 2026-10-18: native matches SDL; 6g, SDL out with a build of the last SDL commit kept (`sdlref`), done in session 24, 2026-10-18, §3.1 #17; 6f's user half against `sdlref`, with the Cmd+Tab fix for exclusive fullscreen with a mode switch, done in session 25, 2026-10-18, §3.1 #18; approved by the user in the reorder, §3.1 #8).**
- First, the baseline: SDL's input latency with the probe (`port/latency.sh`, takes the mouse, so with the user), in the display modes of §2.
- Then native `NSWindow` + `CAMetalLayer` view, keyboard (NSEvent → Q3 keys, text input), raw mouse (GCMouse deltas, cursor capture; moved here from the Phase 5 latency list), ~~gamepad (GCController)~~ (dropped by the user, §3.1 #12), clipboard, window modes, Retina, ~~the `openmohaa://` URL handler~~ (removed instead, the user, §3.1 #13), the color cursor.
- The native input should give precise input timestamps (event/GCMouse timestamps on the host clock) for the probe.
- **Done when:** no SDL in `otool -L` (done, session 24), input matches or beats SDL on the probe (the synthetic mouse: done, session 23) and in the user's feel test at the end (no mouse acceleration), menus/console/text input work. *Met in session 25: GCMouse equal to the movement events on one clock, the feel test "The same", "Same speed, no accel", menus, console and typing fine ("Session 25 result").*
- **Session 18 result: 6a, survey and design** (2026-10-18; read and plan, no code change). The SDL baseline moves: **the user agreed (2026-10-18, asked in session 18) to measure it later, in one sitting with the native layer's first numbers**, from an SDL flavor kept buildable until the last slice ("How it's built" below).
  - **What SDL does for the game today.** The client binary imports 80 SDL functions (`nm -u`), from these files:
    - **`code/sdl/sdl_mtlimp.m`** (renderer side, `MTLimp_*`):
      - `SDL_Init(VIDEO)`, which also creates the application: SDL's `NSApplication` subclass, the menu bar, its app delegate, `finishLaunching`, and three registered user defaults (`ApplePressAndHoldEnabled` NO, `AppleMomentumScrollSupported` NO, `ApplePersistenceIgnoreState` YES);
      - the window (`SDL_CreateWindow`: hidden, Metal, high-DPI; resizable with `r_allowResize`; borderless with `r_noborder` in a window), placed by `r_displayIndex`/`r_centerWindow`, the old position kept across `vid_restart`;
      - exclusive fullscreen (`SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN`: a borderless window at the shielding level, `SDL_SetWindowDisplayMode` → `CGDisplaySetDisplayMode`, the desktop mode restored at exit) and borderless fullscreen (`SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN_DESKTOP`: a native fullscreen Space);
      - `SDL_Metal_CreateView` and its `CAMetalLayer`, `SDL_Metal_GetDrawableSize` (pixels → `glConfig.vidWidth/vidHeight`), `hasShadow = NO` after `SDL_ShowWindow` in fullscreen;
      - `r_availableModes` (the display's modes, one per size, sorted by aspect, then area), `MTLimp_Minimize`;
      - `vid_restart` = `SDL_QuitSubSystem(VIDEO)` and `SDL_Init(VIDEO)` again; `ri.IN_Init(SDL_window)`;
      - dead: the window icon (`USE_ICON` is never defined, so `SDL_SetWindowIcon(NULL)`; the bundle's `openmohaa.icns` is the icon, and `sdl_icon.h` is unused) and `r_sdlDriver` (ROM, "cocoa", read by nothing).
    - **`code/sdl/sdl_input.c`** (client side, `IN_*`):
      - `IN_Frame`, once per `Com_Frame`, after the frame limiter and before `Com_EventLoop` (`common.c`): the joystick, then mouse capture on or off, then `IN_ProcessEvents`, the `SDL_PollEvent` loop (`SDL_PumpEvents` = Cocoa's `nextEventMatchingMask:untilDate:distantPast` loop with `sendEvent:`, then SDL's own queue), then `in_eventTime`, then the delayed `vid_restart` after a resize. The capture is off with the console or UI up in a window, while loading in a window, and without input focus.
      - Keyboard (`IN_TranslateSDLToQ3Key`): the number row by scancode; a `sym` in [space, delete) is its ASCII value (the layout's unshifted character); the grave scancode is `` ` `` (OPM, for the console); a table of named keys; anything else `K_WORLD_0` + scancode (≤ 95); Cmd is `K_COMMAND`; `cl_consoleKeys` ("~ \` 0x7e 0x60") → `K_CONSOLE`. Repeats are dropped unless a key catcher is up. `SE_CHAR` also for backspace (^H), enter/tab/escape (OPM) and Ctrl+letter. `in_keyboardDebug` prints each key.
      - Text: `SDL_StartTextInput` is always on; `SDL_TEXTINPUT` UTF-8 → UTF-32 `SE_CHAR`, a console character becomes a `K_CONSOLE` press and release, and the character right after a console key is dropped.
      - Mouse: relative mode plus window grab; `in_mouse` (0 = off), `in_nograb` (windowed only; fullscreen is refused with it). `SDL_MOUSEMOTION` → `SE_MOUSE` (xrel, yrel); buttons left/middle/right/X1/X2 → `K_MOUSE1`–5, more → `K_AUX`; the wheel → `K_MWHEELUP`/`DOWN` press and release. Releasing the capture warps to the center; `IN_GobbleMotionEvents` drops pending motion when the capture changes.
      - Window events: resized → `r_customwidth/height` and `vid_restart` after 1 s; minimized/restored → `com_minimized`; focus → `com_unfocused`; `SDL_QUIT` (close button, ⌘Q through SDL's app delegate) → `quit Closed window`.
      - Gamepad and joystick: `SDL_Init(JOYSTICK)` always, even with `in_joystick 0` (the default), so SDL's joystick subsystem and its HID manager run anyway; the game controller subsystem only with `in_joystick 1`. `in_availableJoysticks`; a gamepad → `K_PAD0_*` buttons and axes (`joy_threshold` dead zone; `in_joystickUseAnalog` → `SE_JOYSTICK_AXIS` through the `+forward`… binds); a plain joystick → `K_JOY1`–32 buttons, hats, balls (`SE_MOUSE`) and axes; hot plug re-inits.
      - `SDL_DROPFILE` with an `openmohaa:` URL → `Sys_ParseProtocolUri` → `connect`: **compiled out.** `PROTOCOL_HANDLER` is never defined (upstream's CMake rewrite, e556f58c, kept `PROTOCOL_HANDLER_SCHEME` for the Info.plist but not the C define), so the app registers `openmohaa://` and then ignores the URL; `--uri` too.
    - **`code/sdl/sdl_mouse.c`:**
      - `IN_GetMousePosition` (`SDL_GetMouseState`: the absolute position in window points, which `CL_UpdateMouse` compares with pixel sizes);
      - `IN_SetCursorFromImage` (an SDL color cursor from the UI's RGBA image, hotspot 0,0; the image is never freed, an upstream leak), `IN_FreeCursor`;
      - `IN_IsCursorActive` (= relative mode on).
    - **`code/sys/sys_main.c`:** the SDL include; `SDL_WasInit(VIDEO)` in `Sys_In_Restart_f`; `SDL_Quit` in `Sys_Exit`; `Sys_GetProcessorFeatures` from `SDL_HasRDTSC/3DNow/MMX/SSE/SSE2/AltiVec` (all false on arm64, so it returns 0); the SDL version check at the start of `main`.
    - **`code/sys/new/sys_main_new.c`:** `Sys_GetWholeClipboard`/`Sys_SetClipboard` (`SDL_Get/SetClipboardText`; `sys_main.c`'s `Sys_GetClipboardData` is a NULL stub).
    - **`code/sys/sys_loadlib.h`:** `Sys_LoadLibrary` = `SDL_LoadObject` = **`dlopen(path, RTLD_NOW | RTLD_LOCAL)`** (read from SDL's binary: `mov w1, #0x6`), error "Failed loading %s: %s" with `dlerror()`; `SDL_LoadFunction` = `dlsym`, retried with a leading underscore. The `DEDICATED` branch already calls `dlopen(f, RTLD_NOW)` (global on macOS).
    - **`code/sys/sys_local.h`:** `SDL_version.h` and `MINSDL_*`.
    - **Build:** `cmake/libraries/sdl.cmake`, `client.cmake` (`sdl_input.c`, `sdl_mouse.c`), `renderer_metal.cmake` (`sdl_mtlimp.m`), `macos.cmake` (the arm64 thinning of `CLIENT_DEPLOY_LIBRARIES`), `code/thirdparty/SDL2-2.32.8/include`, `thirdparty/libs/macos/libSDL2-2.0.0.dylib` and `libSDL2main.a` (linked but unused on macOS; ld warns about it twice), glcheck's SDL exception.
    - **Already native, staying:** `sys_osx.m`'s `Sys_Dialog` (NSAlert; it works without SDL) and the probe's NSEvent monitor (it goes once the pump records event times itself); `sys_unix.c`'s `Sys_ErrorDialog` calls `Sys_Dialog`.
  - **Findings that shape the design** (from SDL's arm64 binary with `otool -tV`, the SDK headers and session 14's profile):
    1. **The mouse deltas are the system cursor's accelerated deltas, cut to integers.** `Cocoa_HandleMouseEvent` passes `(int)[event deltaX]` (`fcvtzs`, toward zero) to `SDL_SendMouseMotion`. So macOS pointer acceleration applies (this Mac has the system default: acceleration on, `com.apple.mouse.linear`/`scaling` unset), and every fractional part is dropped per event: slow, fine aim below one point per event is lost (±0.9 → 0). GCMouse's `mouseMovedHandler` gives the "raw mouse delta … not affected by mouse sensitivity settings" (SDK header).
    2. **The bundled SDL2 is an unoptimized build** (every function spills its arguments to the stack: `-O0` code), and it brings 18 frameworks into the process (Carbon, IOKit, ForceFeedback, CoreHaptics, AVFoundation, CoreAudio, AudioToolbox, GameController, …).
    3. **Its per-frame cost is small:** in session 14's looping-timedemo profile, 158 of 16618 main-thread samples are under `IN_Frame` (~1%: ~6–10 µs per frame), mostly the run loop's mach port calls, which a native pump makes too. The native layer won't change throughput; its value is the input path itself (raw deltas, no acceleration, no truncation, event times) and leanness.
    4. **SDL minimizes an exclusive-fullscreen window on focus loss** (`SDL_VIDEO_MINIMIZE_ON_FOCUS_LOSS` unset = "auto": exclusive fullscreen, not a Space) and restores the desktop mode; borderless fullscreen (a Space) is left alone.
    5. **`port/latency.sh`'s synthetic mouse can't reach GCMouse.** `mousemove.c` posts CGEvents at the HID event tap, where events enter the WindowServer; GCMouse reads the HID devices below it. So the probe measures the NSEvent path (and SDL) as before, but GCMouse only with a real mouse: the user moves theirs during the probe run. (Expected from the APIs; 6d checks it once.)
    6. **`openmohaa://` is registered but inert** (above). *Removed in session 22 (§3.1 #13).*
    7. **HiDPI input bugs today** (2× displays only; the main display is 1×): the resize check compares points with pixels; the windowed UI's absolute cursor is in points against a pixel-sized UI; the warp to the center passes pixels as points; relative deltas are points while the fullscreen UI cursor moves in pixels (half speed at 2×).
  - **The native design.** New files in `code/macos/` (upstream merges then conflict only on the deleted SDL files, as with 7a): `macos_app.m` (the application, the pump, clipboard, library loading), `macos_window.m` (renderer side: the same `MTLimp_*` functions as `sdl_mtlimp.m`), `macos_input.m` (client side: the same `IN_*` functions as `sdl_input.c` and `sdl_mouse.c`), and a small `macos_local.h` between them. About 1.5k lines of Objective-C (ARC).
    - **The run loop:**
      - `NSApplication` without `[NSApp run]`, created once: regular activation policy, a minimal menu bar (the app menu with Hide, Hide Others, Show All, Quit ⌘Q), `finishLaunching`, activate. The delegate's `applicationShouldTerminate:` queues `quit` and returns `NSTerminateCancel` (the engine exits through `Sys_Quit`). The URL handler (Apple event `kInternetEventClass`/`kAEGetURL`) is installed before `finishLaunching`, under `PROTOCOL_HANDLER`.
      - The three user defaults SDL registers: without `ApplePressAndHoldEnabled` NO, holding a key opens the accent popup instead of repeating; without `ApplePersistenceIgnoreState` YES, macOS restores windows.
      - One pump per `Com_Frame` in `IN_Frame`, where SDL's is: `nextEventMatchingMask:NSEventMaskAny untilDate:nil inMode:NSDefaultRunLoopMode dequeue:YES` until empty, each event handled and passed to `sendEvent:` where AppKit needs it, in one `@autoreleasepool`. Never a blocking wait. The pump also drains the main dispatch queue (GameController's notifications), as SDL's does.
      - `vid_restart` destroys and recreates only the window; the application and the input devices stay.
    - **The window:**
      - `NSWindow` with a layer-hosting content view (`view.layer = metalLayer; view.wantsLayer = YES`) that accepts first responder and is the text input client (below). The `CAMetalLayer` settings are unchanged (opaque, the drawable count, `displaySyncEnabled`, `presentsWithTransaction` NO); `drawableSize` = content size × `backingScaleFactor`, `contentsScale` = `backingScaleFactor`.
      - A window: titled, closable, miniaturizable (resizable with `r_allowResize`), borderless with `r_noborder`; `r_displayIndex`, `r_centerWindow`, the old position kept across `vid_restart`.
      - Exclusive fullscreen (`r_fullscreen 1`): a borderless window at `CGShieldingWindowLevel()` over the screen, no shadow, dock and menu bar hidden (`presentationOptions`). `CGDisplaySetDisplayMode` only when `r_mode`'s size differs from the current mode (SDL switched even to the same mode and back at exit: one display reconfiguration fewer), restored at exit and on focus loss, where the window is minimized as SDL does (finding 4).
      - Borderless fullscreen (`r_fullscreen 1` + `r_noborder 1`): the native fullscreen Space (`NSWindowCollectionBehaviorFullScreenPrimary`, `toggleFullScreen:`), like `SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN_DESKTOP`. The window is created at the desktop size, so the drawable is right before the Space's (asynchronous) animation. **Experiment in 6b:** a borderless window over the screen at the normal level with the dock and menu bar hidden by `presentationOptions` (session 6 tried this without hiding the menu bar: "display rejected"). If it goes direct to display, alt-tab and `vid_restart` skip the Space animation. Keep whichever goes direct (`display_trace.py`).
      - Retina: `glConfig.vidWidth/vidHeight` stay the drawable's pixels; every window coordinate the input uses is converted with `backingScaleFactor` (fixes finding 7); a backing-scale change (the window moved between the 1× and 2× displays) queues `vid_restart` like a resize.
      - `r_availableModes` from `CGDisplayCopyAllDisplayModes`, built like SDL's (same sizes, format and order): 6b compares the startup log's "Available modes" line with SDL's on this Mac.
      - Focus: `windowDidBecomeKey`/`ResignKey` → `com_unfocused`; miniaturize → `com_minimized`; the close button → `quit Closed window`; `MTLimp_Minimize` → miniaturize.
    - **Keyboard and text:**
      - Key events are handled in the pump (`keyDown`/`keyUp`/`flagsChanged`), as SDL does; the content view swallows `keyDown:` so nothing beeps, and ⌘ key equivalents still reach the menu through `sendEvent:`.
      - The `kVK_*` virtual key codes (physical positions) map to SDL's scancode numbering (USB HID usage IDs; a fixed 128-entry table written from the `kVK` and HID usage definitions), so `K_WORLD_0` + scancode and every other key resolve exactly as now, and saved binds keep working. Including SDL's ISO swap (`kVK_ISO_Section` and `kVK_ANSI_Grave` on ISO keyboards).
      - The layout character (`sym`): `UCKeyTranslate` with the current keyboard layout (`TISCopyCurrentKeyboardLayoutInputSource`, rebuilt when the input source changes), unshifted, no dead keys, as SDL builds its keymap. That needs Carbon (HIToolbox) linked again, which 7b dropped from our link; SDL links it today, so it isn't new to the process.
      - Modifiers from `flagsChanged` with the device-dependent left/right bits → `K_SHIFT`/`K_CTRL`/`K_ALT`/`K_COMMAND` down and up per side, like SDL's LSHIFT/RSHIFT; caps lock reported as SDL reports it (checked in 6c).
      - Repeat from `isARepeat`, dropped without a key catcher, as now. `in_keyboardDebug` prints the key code, scancode, character and Q3 key.
      - Text: each `keyDown` also goes through `interpretKeyEvents:` on the content view, a minimal `NSTextInputClient` (dead keys and input methods work); `insertText:` → `SE_CHAR` (UTF-32) with `sdl_input.c`'s console handling; `doCommandBySelector:` ignored (the key went in already); marked text stays out of the game (SDL's `SDL_TEXTEDITING` is ignored today too).
      - **Checked without taking the user's keyboard:** an in-process replay that posts NSEvents into the app's own queue (`[NSApp postEvent:atStart:]`; nothing leaves the process), driven by a cfg in a scratch build of both flavors, compares the `SE_KEY`/`SE_CHAR` streams of SDL and native.
    - **Mouse:**
      - **`in_mouseRaw`** (new, archived): 1 = GCMouse raw deltas; 0 = the NSEvent deltas cut toward zero per event exactly as SDL does (the like-for-like baseline, and the fallback). **Default 0 until the user's feel test**: raw deltas change aim feel and sensitivity (no acceleration; device counts instead of points), so the user picks the default then (planned: 1, the "no mouse acceleration" of Done when). *Picked by the user before 6f: 1 (§3.1 #14).*
      - GCMouse: watched through `GCMouseDidConnect`/`Disconnect`/`BecomeCurrent`; `handlerQueue` = a serial dispatch queue at `QOS_CLASS_USER_INTERACTIVE`; `mouseMovedHandler` adds the deltas (float sums, fractions carried over) and the times into an accumulator under an `os_unfair_lock`; `IN_Frame` takes it once per pump (one `SE_MOUSE`). Used only while captured and focused; the UI cursor uses NSEvents. 6d checks the y sign (GameController's y may point up) and `lastEventTimestamp`'s clock (undocumented: compared with `CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW` once; used as the event time if it is that clock, else the handler's own time).
      - Capture (= SDL's relative mode plus grab): warp the cursor to the window center in global points (`CGWarpMouseCursorPosition`, with `CGEventSourceSetLocalEventsSuppressionInterval` 0 so a warp doesn't freeze input for 0.25 s), `CGAssociateMouseAndMouseCursorPosition(false)`, `[NSCursor hide]`; release reverses it. Same rules as `IN_Frame` now, with `in_mouse` and `in_nograb`.
      - Buttons and wheel: NSEvent (`buttonNumber` → `K_MOUSE1`–5 and `K_AUX`, as SDL numbers them; the wheel's sign → the wheel keys; momentum off). 6d also logs GCMouse's button times next to the NSEvent times of the same clicks: if the GameController path is measurably earlier (≥ ~0.2 ms), buttons move to GCMouse while captured.
      - UI cursor: windowed menus read the absolute position (`mouseLocationOutsideOfEventStream` → view → pixels: fixes the 2× bug); captured menus (fullscreen) take NSEvent deltas × `backingScaleFactor` (the same feel on 1×).
      - The color cursor: an `NSCursor` from an `NSImage` of the RGBA pixels, hotspot 0,0 (and the image freed: the upstream leak).
      - The probe: event times come straight from the pump (`NSEvent.timestamp`) and from the GCMouse accumulator, so `Sys_MouseEventMonitor` goes; `CL_LatencyPump` stays at the pump start. Enabled for later (Phase 5 timing work, needs the user): a view-only GCMouse read right before the view is built (JayRewd's late mouse idea, §4b).
    - **Gamepad, clipboard, dialogs, the rest:**
      - **Gamepads and joysticks: dropped** (the user, 2026-10-18, §3.1 #12). The next two items are the design as it was:
      - `GCController` (connect/disconnect notifications, `in_joystick`, `in_joystickNo`, `in_availableJoysticks`): the extended gamepad is read in `IN_Frame` as SDL polls its controller. Buttons → `K_PAD0_*` (A/B/X/Y; options = back, home = guide, menu = start; the thumbstick buttons, shoulders, d-pad; paddles, touchpad and the Xbox share button where the profile has them). Sticks and triggers → the same keys and `SE_JOYSTICK_AXIS` code, values scaled to ±32767, y flipped to SDL's down-positive.
      - **Plain HID joysticks** (not gamepads: `K_JOY*`, hats, balls) have no GameController equivalent. Proposed: drop them (`in_joystick` defaults to 0); an IOHIDManager path only if the user wants one (asked at 6f; settled by §3.1 #12: none).
      - Clipboard: `NSPasteboard` (`stringForType:NSPasteboardTypeString`; `clearContents` + `setString:forType:`), the same 4096-byte limit.
      - Dialogs: `Sys_Dialog` stays; it releases the cursor capture first, and leaves exclusive fullscreen if a window exists.
      - `Sys_LoadLibrary` = `dlopen(f, RTLD_NOW | RTLD_LOCAL)` (SDL's flags), `Sys_LoadFunction` = `dlsym` (no underscore retry: `dlsym` takes C names), `Sys_LibraryError` = `dlerror()`; the `DEDICATED` branch unchanged.
      - `sys_main.c`: no SDL version check, no `SDL_Quit`; `Sys_GetProcessorFeatures` returns 0 (what SDL's six queries give on arm64); `Sys_In_Restart_f` checks that a window exists.
      - ~~`openmohaa://`: the Apple-event handler under `#ifdef PROTOCOL_HANDLER`, so turning it on is one CMake line (`PROTOCOL_HANDLER="openmohaa"`). It stays off, as upstream has it, unless the user asks (asked at 6f).~~ Removed with the scheme instead (the user, §3.1 #13).
      - Not in Phase 6: `NSActivityLatencyCritical`, the main thread's QoS and Game Mode on/off (§4b rule 5) are Phase 5 measurements with the user.
    - **What stays:** `glconfig_t` (unchanged); refimport/refexport (`IN_Init` still takes a `void *`, now the `NSWindow`); every cvar and its meaning (`r_mode`, `r_fullscreen`, `r_noborder`, `r_allowResize`, `r_centerWindow`, `r_displayIndex`, `r_metalDrawables`, `r_swapInterval`, `in_mouse`, `in_nograb`, `in_keyboardDebug`, `cl_consoleKeys`; `in_joystick*` and `joy_threshold` only until 6g, with SDL: §3.1 #12); `r_sdlDriver` (read by nothing) goes. The frame order, pacing and usercmd timing don't change.
  - **How it's built and compared:** a CMake option `USE_SDL` (ON until the last slice) picks SDL's files or `code/macos/`'s, and `port/build.sh native` builds the native flavor from the same tree. The `metal` flavor stays SDL, unchanged, until the switch, so the bench, the fixed views and the probe compare both in one sitting (the user's OK above). `port/latency.sh` gets an `OMM_FLAVOR` switch (it always runs `metal` now). The last slice removes SDL and the option; `metal` is then the native build.
  - **Slices** (each with both flavors built, `port/glcheck.sh`, `bench.sh run metal 3` and `run native 3`, and the native smoke test of what it covers):

Checked without input: the timedemo bench, `port/shot.sh regress` on the native flavor (identical images), each display mode starting and quitting, direct to display in both fullscreen modes (Instruments trace), the modes list against SDL's.
    - **6c, keyboard and text:** the key map, layout characters, modifiers, repeat, the console key, text input; the in-process replay against SDL.
    - **6d, the mouse:** both `in_mouseRaw` paths, capture, buttons and wheel, the UI cursor, the color cursor, HiDPI, the probe's times. The NSEvent path is checked with in-process posted events; GCMouse needs a real mouse (the user, or 6f).
    - **6e, the rest:** `Sys_Dialog` leaving exclusive fullscreen and the capture, ~~the URL handler (off)~~ (`openmohaa://` removed, §3.1 #13), whatever is left; the native flavor is then complete. (Gamepads were planned here; dropped, §3.1 #12.)
    - **6f, with the user:** the latency sitting, SDL (`metal`) against native: the synthetic mouse with `in_mouseRaw 0` in the three display modes at `com_maxfps 250`, then `in_mouseRaw 1` with the user's own mouse; the user's feel test; ~~the `in_mouseRaw` default, `openmohaa://`~~ (decided before 6f: §3.1 #13, #14).
    - **6g, SDL out:** `USE_SDL`, `code/sdl/` (with SDL's joystick and game controller code, §3.1 #12), the vendored SDL headers and libraries, `sdl.cmake`, glcheck's exception, the probe's NSEvent monitor; `otool -L` without SDL; bench, `regress base-s9`, skinab; push. 6g waits for 6f. If the user wants SDL gone before they can do 6f, a `metalref` build of the last SDL commit keeps the baseline measurable (with their OK). *Done in session 24 before the user's half of 6f, their OK (§3.1 #17), with the last SDL commit kept as its own flavor, `sdlref`.*
- **Session 19 result: 6b, the application and the window** (2026-10-18, the user away most of the session; commits e82a1d08, f87f8820 and the docs).
  - **Built:** `USE_SDL` (CMake option in `cmake/platforms/macos.cmake`, default ON; `sdl.cmake` returns early without it and defines `USE_SDL` for the client otherwise); `port/build.sh native` (`-DUSE_SDL=OFF`, its own build and install trees). `client.cmake` and `renderer_metal.cmake` pick the files through `CLIENT_INPUT_SOURCES` and `RENDERER_METAL_WINDOW_SOURCE`, in the same place in the source lists, so the SDL build's object order is unchanged. The shared sys files keep SDL's code in `#ifdef USE_SDL` branches: **`sys_main.c` and `sys_main_new.c` (with `sys_local.h` and `sys_loadlib.h`) preprocess identically before and after for the SDL client and for `omohaaded`** (`clang -E -P` with the build's own flags, both trees laid out alike). So the `metal` flavor is the same SDL code, the baseline for 6f.
  - **`code/macos/` (≈1.1k lines, ARC):**
    - `macos_local.h`: the interface between the three files.
    - `macos_app.m`: `NSApplication` without `run` (regular policy, SDL's three user defaults, the application menu: Hide ⌘H, Hide Others ⌥⌘H, Show All, Quit ⌘Q), a delegate whose `applicationShouldTerminate:` records the quit and returns `NSTerminateCancel`, `finishLaunching`, `activateIgnoringOtherApps:YES` (SDL's call; only "to be deprecated", no warning), an `NSActivityIdleDisplaySleepDisabled` activity (SDL suspends the screen saver with the same kind of power assertion), the pump (`nextEventMatchingMask:… untilDate:nil … dequeue:YES` + `sendEvent:` until empty, one `@autoreleasepool`), the `NSPasteboard` clipboard. Not copied from SDL: its first-pump activation hack (activate the Dock, **`SDL_Delay(300)`**, then the app, read from its `applicationDidFinishLaunching:`), which fixes a menu bar that ignores clicks after a terminal launch. The native app was focused at once in every run (`com_unfocused 0`); whether the menu bar takes clicks after a terminal launch is for the user's look in 6f.
    - `macos_window.m`: the `MTLimp_*` functions, log lines and cvars of `sdl_mtlimp.m` (`r_sdlDriver` gone). An `NSWindow` subclass that can become key when borderless, a layer-hosting content view (`view.layer = CAMetalLayer` before `wantsLayer`; it swallows key events so AppKit doesn't beep), a window delegate (the close button → `[NSApp terminate:]`, i.e. the same quit as ⌘Q; exclusive fullscreen's focus handling; the layer's `contentsScale` on a backing change) and an application-activation observer that deminiaturizes the window (SDL's `focusSomeWindow`). Displays in SDL's order for `r_displayIndex` (the main one, then the others, no mirrors). A window is centered on its display (SDL2 centers `SDL_WINDOWPOS_UNDEFINED` too, so `r_centerWindow` changes nothing in either), or keeps its old top-left across `vid_restart`. Exclusive fullscreen: a borderless window at `CGShieldingWindowLevel()` over the display, no shadow, the dock and menu bar hidden while it is up; the display mode changes only when r_mode's mode isn't the current one (chosen as `SDL_GetClosestDisplayMode` chooses: the smallest mode at least that large in the desktop's format, the requested or desktop refresh, HiDPI before 1×), captured and faded as SDL does it (all displays when it is the main one); on focus loss the desktop mode comes back and the window is minimized, as SDL does. Borderless fullscreen: a titled, resizable window at the desktop size with `FullScreenPrimary`, then `toggleFullScreen:` (a Space). `r_borderlessWindow 1` (archived, latched): borderless fullscreen as a borderless window over the screen instead of a Space (the experiment, below). Windows are created relative to their screen (`initWithContentRect:…screen:`), as SDL creates them; the Space asks for the dock and menu bar hidden (`window:willUseFullScreenPresentationOptions:`), as SDL does. Retina: the drawable is the content size × `backingScaleFactor` (the mode's pixel scale after a mode switch).
    - `macos_input.m`: the `IN_*` functions as a stub layer: the pump in `IN_Frame` (where SDL's was, after `CL_LatencyPump`), the window's events through notifications observed on the main thread inside the pump (focus → `com_unfocused`, miniaturize → `com_minimized`, a resize or backing change → `r_customwidth/height` in points and `vid_restart` after 1 s, comparing **pixels with pixels**: finding 7's resize bug is fixed), the quit request → `quit Closed window` after the pump (EXEC_NOW, as SDL). The input cvars are registered as SDL registers them. No keys, mouse, cursor or gamepads yet (the cursor image is freed at once, not leaked).
    - sys: `dlopen(f, RTLD_NOW | RTLD_LOCAL)`/`dlsym`/`dlerror`; no SDL version check, `SDL_Quit` or CPU feature queries (`Sys_GetProcessorFeatures` returns 0, as SDL's six queries do on arm64); `in_restart` checks `MacOS_WindowExists()`.

The same within the noise (the first native run is the usual first-run high; p99 needs session 17's 8+ alternating runs to compare). Measured on the e82a1d08 build; f87f8820 only changes where windows are created and the Space's presentation options. Worth noting: every SDL run had a 5.0–5.9 ms max frame, every native run 1.2–1.3 ms.

    - Windowed: starts focused, `vid_restart`, quits.
    - **Every display mode starts, `vid_restart`s or plays, and quits cleanly** on the native flavor (windows with `nat_q.cfg`; fullscreen in dm/mohdm6 at 250 fps through `port/latency.sh` with `MOUSE=0 LATSTATS=0 TRACE=1`: no input, the probe off, 6 s of Metal System Trace, `display_trace.py`; the window list from `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`, a scratch tool `build.noindex/s19/winlist`).

The first native build opened the display-1 window on the main display: `initWithContentRect:…` without `screen:` takes the rect relative to the main screen; created relative to its screen as SDL does (f87f8820).

      | Native mode | Swaps with a game frame | Direct | Game Mode |
      |---|---|---|---|
      | exclusive, desktop size (1920×1080, no mode change) | 1681 and 1531 (two runs) | all | on |
      | exclusive, 1280×720 (**mode switched**: 1280×720 @ 240 Hz, all displays captured as SDL captures them; SDL the same sitting: direct, all 1395) | 1367 | all | — |

      The modes come back at exit (`system_profiler`: 1920×1080 @ 240 and the 4K's 1920×1080 @ 60).
    - **The Space first went "display rejected" on every frame**: the window sat at 0,30, 1920×1050, below the menu bar. This Mac has `AppleMenuBarVisibleInFullscreen` 1 (the menu bar stays in fullscreen apps); SDL's Space covered it (0,0 1920×1080, direct, the same sitting) because SDL's window delegate answers `window:willUseFullScreenPresentationOptions:` with full screen + hide dock + hide menu bar. The native delegate does the same now (f87f8820): direct on every frame.
    - **The borderless-window experiment works:** a borderless window over the screen at the normal level, no shadow, with `presentationOptions` hiding the dock and menu bar, goes direct on every frame and turns Game Mode on (session 6's "display rejected" was the menu bar on top). It skips the Space's animation when switching away and at `vid_restart`, and other applications' windows show over it when it isn't in front. **Both go direct, so the choice is the user's** (asked at 6f; *decided in session 21: the Space, §3.1 #15*): the Space stays the default (SDL's behavior, what the user played 3d in), and `r_borderlessWindow 1` (archived, latched) picks the window.
